ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-12-sejm-49-3.u5	Thank you, Member.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-12-sejm-49-3.u7	Please respond to the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Environment, Mr Sławomir Mazurka. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-12-sejm-49-3.u8	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Monitoring of waste management activities is carried out by the Environmental Protection Inspection, which supervises the activities of entities operating in the sector in the framework of planning and intervention controls. All cases of illegal waste management reported by both economic operators, natural persons and other state administrations are under the constant supervision of voivodship environmental inspectors. At the same time, it is worth noting that control powers for the execution of waste management permits also have the authorities that issued them, i.e. - as mentioned here by the Member - local governments, especially the starosts, the marshals of voivodships, but also the authorities of the state, i.e. regional environmental directors. Local authorities have legal instruments from the Waste Act to prevent cases of illegal storage, storage of waste and in the event of ongoing disposal. However, due to the calls made, the Ministry of the Environment is working to propose changes in the near future which will improve this situation and present the appropriate financial mechanism. The environmental resort shall carry out activities to seal the waste management system and to eliminate the pathologies associated with illegal waste management. In the coming years, these actions will include changes in waste management legislation, waste law, including in particular municipal waste, cleanliness and order law in municipalities, which has just been submitted for government work. These proposals will be complemented by changes to the rules on extended producer responsibility for packaging, batteries, batteries and waste electrical, electronic equipment, which will also result from the negotiated circular economy package. A number of these actions and this increased responsibility for the introductions will be defined. Of course, we are already working in the Committee on Legal Affairs before the Council of Ministers. There's a bill to amend the Waste Act. These actions will be aimed at improving enforcement efficiency both by the control authorities and by the authorities issuing administrative decisions on waste management. These penalties, which you asked for, administratively, are up to PLN 1 million. The proposed amendments to the Waste Act provide for the keeping of waste records only via an electronic database on products and packaging and on waste management. It's going to be this base, so-called. BDO in brief. The project is also planned to give up the aggregate waste transfer card. These changes will enable ongoing on-line control and not, as so far, periodic control. Hence, when inspectors arrive after 7 days, the printers are still warm. So we want to avoid this so that this audit and these reporting activities are carried out on an ongoing basis. It is also a matter of verifying the data relating to the transport of waste by listing the data contained in the waste transfer sheet set up in the database. This instrument will be used both during the control of specific transport and remotely by the competent authorities. It will also be possible to identify some risks, where they are the greatest, to address, even though limited, environmental inspection measures. In the case of waste management, changes are envisaged in order to seal the collection and marketing system by introducing into the system a confirmation of each acquisition of these waste by the authorised entity and the settlement of the mass of waste received especially from the areas of the municipalities in the daily system by the so-called. transfer cards for municipal waste. Such a form of drawing up of documents used for waste records, which will be integrated into the BDO, will also allow the authorities of the Environmental Protection Inspections to have access to the documentation kept by the recipients of municipal waste. We are considering proposing further legislative amendments aimed at sealing the existing rules and facilitating supervision and control of waste activities. We are also considering introducing - I have already said this from this point on - a financial guarantee mechanism for entrepreneurs operating in the field of waste management in order to prevent waste dumping. Today the local government actually makes decisions and it goes to the barn, right, to the so-called. pole. So these illegal practices that take place in other areas of the economy are now also focused on waste management. This is also an attempt to avoid appropriate charges. In some cases, such entities have been requested to permit waste management, but their property situation was not good enough to be able to do anything later on with the waste. We also want and we are struggling with the problem... This is often reported by inspectors, i.e. those who are constantly involved in waste management. These are the famous 7-day controls. This is a big challenge, which is very strongly communicated by environmental inspectors. We're gonna talk about it and encourage it to... It is not about restricting entrepreneurs, restricting entrepreneurship, but about ensuring that honest entrepreneurs can work so that this competitive advantage and optimizing economic activity at the expense of citizens does not take place at the expense of the state. This change is necessary in our view, because the necessity of this notification also makes these controls de facto ineffective. When cases are reported on an ongoing basis, implementations are carried out by environmental inspectors. These checks shall be carried out, [[♪ The bell ♪]] We'll be heading in that direction. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-12-sejm-49-3.u17	Thank you very much. Mr Jakub Skiba, Secretary of State at the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration, answers. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-12-sejm-49-3.u18	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The decision of the Government of the Republic of Poland to continue the suspension of the local border traffic agreement with the Russian Federation was and is dictated by national security considerations... [[Like what?]] ...and the need to reduce risks and to counter all kinds of threats that could prevent the development and freedom to pursue the interests of the Polish state. [[Specifically.]] The intention of the movement with the Russian Federation was to facilitate the establishment and strengthening of social and economic relations between border residents. However, the use of this instrument must be based on fundamental trust, goodwill and principles of cooperation. Our services continuously monitor threats to the security of Poland. One of the negative developments that have intensified over the past few years, especially recently, in connection with the exercises of the Russian armed forces Zapad, is the increasing militarisation of the Kaliningrad region. It is accompanied by the process of settling in this area of military personnel and by increased activity of special forces and other Russian forces departments. In addition, attention should be paid to other potentially hazardous hybrid activities, including: in intelligence activities, provocations, cyber attacks and economic pressure. Key projects in the field of external security in Poland include: the aim of strengthening the so-called the eastern flanks of the North Atlantic Treaty Organisation or Poland's efforts within the European Union to maintain a firm policy course towards Russia in connection with its actions in Ukraine. I would like to point out that in Elbląg, very close to the Polish border with the Kaliningrad circuit, from July this year. The Northern-East Multinational Division is stationed coordinating NATO forces on the eastern flank. That's the answer to security. On the other hand, it is important to stress the other things that refute the thesis that you have raised. Firstly, it is not true that the border traffic with Russia has decreased. Statistics, Border Guard data absolutely don't support that. Namely, in July and August 2016, the border exceeded around 250,000 respectively. Russian citizens, in July and August 2017, amounted to 370 000. The total passenger traffic has been rebuilt on the basis of visas, and the issue of visas gives a much better opportunity to check who is in our country. The arguments concerning the economic dimension are also not true. Namely hard data of the Central Statistical Office and National Tax Administration clearly show that the total value of spending of foreigners, i.e. Russians coming to Poland from the Kaliningrad region, amounted to PLN 270 million in the first half of 2017 and increased by 14.2% compared to the same period of 2016, when it amounted to PLN 237 million. Similar figures are indicated by the National Tax Administration. On the basis of the tax free accounts in May and June 2017, around 8 and 9 thousand were registered. the accounts for an amount correspondingly higher by about PLN 10 Economic activity in these voivodships did not suffer at all, but gained. Who's lost on this? The persons who carried the excise goods across the border lost on this. That is the truth and there are hard data that clearly show it. This is also demonstrated by data on the number of offences related to the smuggling of fuel, cigarettes, alcohol, which occurred in fewer criminal, fiscal and parking proceedings. In May 2016, before the suspension, approximately 2200 of these crimes were registered a month, now we have a level of 1600.1800. Another example of what the economic sense of this border traffic was all about. I would like to point out that the suspension of local border traffic has certainly had a positive impact on the increased collection of VAT, which is registered by our fiscal service. Finally, the world championship issue. It's not true, a little border traffic isn't meant for fans to cross the border. There will be only four group matches in Kaliningrad. It is not known whether Poland will perform. It's like he has nothing to do with it. The Russian Federation will certainly adopt extra-ordinary solutions that will facilitate the movement of fans into the territory of the Russian Federation, these matters are planned. A small border traffic doesn't do that. The sense and purpose of the local border traffic are completely different. Thank you very much.

The first tranche of PLN 200 million has already been paid, the second tranche of PLN 300 million is available by 30 November this year, the third tranche of PLN 100 million - by June next year and the fourth tranche of PLN 200 million - by June 2019 Transze the second, third and fourth will be paid in full or in part at the explicit request of public television. Under the agreement, the company was required to provide the President of the Council of Ministers and the Director of the Reprivatisation Fund, the Finance Minister, with quarterly reports on the use of the loan during the period of validity of this agreement, together with the opinion of the Supervisory Board within 30 days of the end of each subsequent calendar quarter. Television signals that it wants to allocate the amount of this loan to statutory activities aimed at carrying out tasks involving technological projects and programming initiatives. And so on. These are investments in the area of production technology and content management. The implementation of projects in this area is aimed at adapting the technological base to the current production needs of the company. They cover to a large extent investments in the production capacity of off-road units. Let us remind you that public television has 16 off-road units. In addition, investments in equipment and infrastructure are envisaged to allow the transfer of production within the company and to inform the production process. The results of the projects are to improve the production process and improve the technical quality of the content produced. The second purpose for which this loan is intended is to develop the programme offer. The company plans to develop a software offer that serves the implementation of the public mission. Investment is planned in programmes that bring Poland's history closer and more popular. In response to the demand of viewers, Polish Television also intends to broadcast the most important sports events. The last famous story related to the purchase of rights to broadcast all matches of Polish football team - starting with those closest to them, which are due to take place in November - especially during the World Cup in Russia. By letter dated 8 May 2017, the Minister of Culture and National Heritage sent a favourable opinion to the Minister of Finance on the impact of this loan on the company's current financial liquidity. In this way, it will be possible to support in particular those districts in which secondary schools run certified military uniform classes, while we also assume that these facilities could be used by military units. Since in the last several years almost half of the military shooting range has been liquidated, Polish troops, the Polish Armed Forces, currently own only fifty objects. As a result, some units must reach the military range about 200 km. Therefore, the possibility of financing and setting up such municipal shooting galleries would also involve the possibility of being used by some military units and the Territorial Defence Army, which, as we know, are built in all powiats from the east to the west of the country. As far as training of Polish Armed Forces' personal reserves is concerned, this is indeed a very important issue. After the suspension of the essential military service, which was not prepared, there was indeed a problem with the training of reserves. At the moment, work is underway in the Ministry of National Defence to change this situation. We should train about 35,000 a year. persons. The worst year was 2012, when 10 times less reservists were trained, but at the moment this situation is improving, but we are also implementing specific programmes to fundamentally change this situation. One of them is the secondary school support program, which conducts certified pawns of military uniform classes. The graduates of these schools will be in reserve after a short training course. The second programme has started since October this year, it is a programme of voluntary training of students of the University Legion. As part of this programme, we have secured nearly 10,000 during the pilot year, i.e. this year. training places. Depending on the reports, it's gonna be 5,000. Petty Officers, or 10,000. Privates. In the last week of September, the average price was PLN 5.1 and was comparable to the prices used in other countries of the European Union. Fewer than in Poland, large pig producers were paid in five countries: Belgium, the Netherlands, France, Denmark and, of course, Estonia. The fall in prices is influenced by population growth, as in Poland it increased by 4.5%. , and in other countries also increased at an average of 1% This year. In August 2017, Poland was concentrated by more than 8% more pigs than in July and 11% more than August. Thus, the overall situation affects the situation of each farmer. With regard to this specific issue raised by the Member in the protection area around the areas of African swine fever, there is no reason for lower prices to be applied by buyers. There are no restrictions on trade in these pigs. Meat of these pigs is full-value meat and can be sold throughout the European market. There are restrictions on trade to third countries. Some countries do not buy meat from Poland at all, some apply their own regulations, in which they exclude entire Polish voivodships, where African swine fever occurred, from the possibility of delivering pork to their markets, but they are markets generally distant and not decisive in terms of prices, about the possibility of selling Polish pork. This type of practice is unacceptable. I would ask you to provide information in which case such use of the farmer's situation has taken place and not to pay the appropriate amount, with reference to the possibility of obtaining support. Support in the protection area does not apply. So-called. The yellow zone is not used because there are no trade restrictions for meat. The label is obtained the same as meat from the white zone, i.e. from the rest of Poland. We will look at this individual case in order to avoid it in the future and to make sure that the purchasers do not apply this type of practice. And here, of course, we are also able to intervene and inform the bet that it has misled its counterparty. Our support measures are targeted at areas at risk and protection, designated around the outbreaks, because this is the most difficult situation, because meat from these areas, of course healthy meat from healthy pigs, tested in accordance with Union and international law, can only go to heat treatment - therefore it actually gets a lower price, but this price is compensated, at least until now it has been compensated, we are working on a system of further compensation - as well as areas where African swine fever occurs in feral pigs or pigs, i.e. areas of the blue zone, where also [[♪ The bell ♪]] We will compensate and compensate farmers for losses in various forms. Thank you very much for this question, because we, as the Ministry, also see the need for changes in the system of financing adult schools. We see the need for these changes in the system for organising and financing adult education and therefore we have taken very concrete measures because our goal is adult education, which will be attractive, effective, modern and flexible, education that responds to the challenges of the 21st century. When preparing solutions in this area, we take into account the extremely important role of adult education in raising the level of education and obtaining professional qualifications. This is one of the key factors determining the preparation of modern staff for the Polish economy. The Polish education system guarantees adults the universal right to education. This training includes learning in adult schools, as well as acquiring and supplementing vocational knowledge, skills and qualifications within courses provided by lifelong learning institutions, practical training facilities and vocational training and training centres. Lifelong learning in general education has different forms of organisation and objectives than vocational education. Adult general education is a dominant form of adult education, mainly second chances for people who for various reasons did not finish primary school, previous secondary school or secondary school before the age of 18. The main link in this sector is a general education school for adults. Most people who choose to study in this type of school want to get a secondary education, graduate. This is important not only for prestigious reasons, but also for them when it comes to increasing opportunities in the labour market. In these schools, there are mainly people whose early secondary education has failed or graduates of primary vocational schools. In turn, the aim of vocational education for adults is primarily to increase their chances in the labour market. The main challenge here is to adapt education to the needs of employers and the labour market. The dynamic development of technology means that education and knowledge acquired at school must be updated, supplemented and expanded during professional work. The changes in qualifications and occupations and their emergence and disappearance in the labour market make the adult school education cycle not only not too long for employers' needs but also for learners' needs. Currently, the only school providing vocational training for adults is a secondary school for those with secondary or secondary education. This school lasts from 1 to 2.5 years depending on the profession. In addition, since the 2020/2021 school year, we have introduced a second-year professional school for first-grade graduates. We therefore ensure the full range of vocational training. We also create this 2nd degree school with the aim of obtaining qualifications in the 2nd degree professional school by adults and we are preparing appropriate changes on this issue. Adults who supplement their professional knowledge and qualifications have different preferences and aspirations than those who choose general education. It is crucial for them to obtain certificates confirming certain professional qualifications, in the form of a certificate or diploma, obtained after passing external examinations. The provision of non-training or exchange-rate education is essential in this case. We therefore make it even more flexible by developing the offer of exchange-rate education. Above all, we take into account the needs and opportunities of adults, who most often have to combine learning with work or care for other family members. At the same time, we intend to allow vocational schools to conduct shorter forms of adult education. With regard to the opinion contained in the question, I would like to assure you that we largely share the comments made by the Members. We see negative developments in the functioning of adult schools and analyse them carefully. We are introducing changes to improve the effectiveness and quality of adult education. At the moment, there is a procedated draft law on the financing of educational tasks, which includes changes that are designed to organize the system of funding for education, so that it is transparent and easier to apply to local governments and school heads. We have also prepared solutions for subsidising adult schools, including a change in the way grants are calculated. One of the key solutions is to make the grant and its amount dependent on the effectiveness of education, i.e. obtaining learning outcomes, i.e. the so-called. a grant for the effect. In the case of general education schools and adult secondary schools, part of the grant will be paid to the pupil, as before, and part will be paid after a passing exam respectively. The amount of the grant will therefore depend in part on whether the person running the school will prove that the student passed the exam. The part of the grant that will be subject to the examination will be defined by appropriate weights of the educational grant. Another solution will be to subsidize only one school [[♪ The bell ♪]] for adults, where the pupil is a pupil of more than one school. Therefore, I think that we are largely responding to the challenges that arise from the reports of the Supreme Audit Chamber, as well as from the observations that we have as the Ministry of Education.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-12-sejm-49-3.u96	Thank you very much. Mr Piotr Dardziński corresponds to the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Science and Higher Education.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-12-sejm-49-3.u97	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Thank you very much for this question, which concerns the important issue of the science system in Poland. As she said, research institutes are the part of the science that is dedicated to cooperation with economic operators and entrepreneurs. Today, when we are trying to change the Polish economy, but in general to implement reforms which are civilised to place Poland among countries that are leaders of progress and change, rather than those who are merely realizers or subcontractors, the importance of research institutes is even more important. Before I answer the question, two words of introduction. What is the problem? The problem is that research institutes are very numerous today, fragmented, even though they have been consolidated for 10 years and are subject to surveillance scattered between 16 ministers. We have 116 institutes, plus a minus, because a few are in the process of insolvency, and they are subject to 16 ministers. In addition, this subsidiary is double, because it is subject in substance to the ministers of the department concerned, while in terms of legal and financial dependency, it is supervised by the Minister of Science and Higher Education, which creates a completely unclear system. The changes that have been carried out in the Research Institutes Act so far aimed at strengthening the role of substantive supervision by individual ministers. On the other hand, the reform we have prepared is different in nature. I said: prepared by us, I used the plural, because the prepared laws are co-led by the Ministry of Development and the Ministry of Science and Higher Education. The first version of the reform was a law on the National Institute of Technology. It provided for the consolidation of all institutes under the supervision of the Minister of Development and certain institutes from other ministries, as decided by the relevant ministers, into one body which was to have one legal basis. After public consultation and taking into account the scientific community's comments, we changed this concept and another law was prepared according to a mechanism recommended by the institutes themselves. A network of research institutes shall be set up in place of a single legal organisation. The most important difference between these concepts is that in the first version the institutes did not have their own legal personality, in the second version they have legal personality. In both cases, our idea is to create a decentralised organisation in which most of the activities are focused at the level of institutes and proper processes are carried out by the Institutes' Directors, while the Centre, in this case the Centre of the Research Network: Łukasiewicz... Because that's what this new organism is called - the Research Network: Łukasiewicz. The establishment of an additional centre within institutes, which will act as coordinator of the activities of individual institutes, will significantly change the ability of entrepreneurs to exploit the potential of these institutes. Because we agree that institutes are not quite making the most of their capabilities, and these opportunities are large. One of the fundamental barriers is that they can work together legally in a difficult way. In other words, if there is an entrepreneur who intends to carry out a research process involving infrastructure and researchers from different institutes, then there is now a very complicated process of mutual coordination, because there is no single entity that could do so. Today, by working with the Research Network: Łukasiewicz, the entrepreneur will have access both to the entire research infrastructure that is located in these institutes and to the staff potential that is located in these institutes, and regardless of which institutes establish this cooperation, he will be able to use the resources of other institutes automatically. That's what I call it. economies of scale, and in this case we are trying creatively to exploit the experiences of our partners, mainly European ones, due to legal conditions, but not only. Most European countries have already carried out this consolidation process. He had different models, sometimes it was the commercialization of research institutes and the appointment, as we proposed, of one organization. In France there is a stage of networking, a bit like we do. In Germany, we are dealing with an association that has set up research institutes. Whatever form this process takes, one thing is important: the work of individual institutes must be coordinated by a centre, this centre should have a minimum range of its own scientific activity, while the maximum level of support for institutes, especially in the area of intellectual property protection, legal support and business support, i.e. commercialisation. And it is as if these three main objectives of the reform are to increase commercialisation, activate the scientific community and protect intellectual property, so that it remains in Poland. If we could implement the Law on the Research Network: Łukasiewicz, there would be a third-largest research network in Europe that could compete with German or French institutes in a real way, in obtaining grants from European funds at European level. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-12-sejm-49-3.u304	Thank you, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mrs Ewa Malik, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-12-sejm-49-3.u305	Mr. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I am pleased to present an opinion on the report of the Committee on Energy and the Treasury on the Government bill on compensation for the loss of the right to free coal, Parliamentary Printing No 1898. The draft law responds to the socially motivated need to redress the pensioners and the mining rencistos, who, from 1 January 2012, due to the deteriorating situation of mining companies, have been declared entitled to the coal depot provided for in the internal regulations. These decisions were taken either unilaterally by the employer's termination of collective agreements guaranteeing this right or by agreement of both parties during the Civil Platform and PSL governments. This was the result of pressure from the management boards of mining companies, which in a coal crisis saw in these decisions the only possibility of seriously reducing the costs of mining units, and which, by mass decision to deprive mining pensioners of the right to a coal debt, left themselves an average salary of 120 000. PLN per month. It was an average that belonged to every president or often a member of the board of directors in the days of the Civic Platform. Thus, the Government of Law and Justice has taken the form of this kind of injustice in the treatment of the right to the coal debt of pensioners and miners, facing the victims of this unequal treatment, has come up with a proposal of one-off redress. The proposed solution is to pay a compensation contribution of 10 000 from the State budget. zł, paid once until the end of 2017 During the reading of the draft law at yesterday's meeting of the Committee on Energy and the Treasury, Members of the Law and Justice made four substantive amendments, including those of particular importance to former miners, as they will contribute to increasing the number of persons to be covered by this law. In addition, the proposed amendments will make the law applicable to persons entitled to mining companies in which, on the basis of collective agreements, agreements and other regulations in force in a mining undertaking, the right to free coal was granted by employers to pensioners and subsequently received by termination, whether by partial or total solution, unilateral or by agreement of the parties to collective agreements, agreements and other regulations. The amendment adopted by the Commission is also an important amendment which provides for an extension of the deadline for the submission of an application for compensation to the competent paying undertaking to be extended to 3 weeks. In conclusion, the Law and Justice Club fully supports the regulations contained in Seym Printing No. 1898 and calls on the High Sejm to support the bill submitted. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-12-sejm-49-3.u345	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the report of the committee on the Government's draft act on compensation for the loss of the right to free coal. The purpose of the bill is to compensate, within the scope of the State budget, for the financial losses incurred by persons who have received free coal in kind or as equivalent on the basis of collective agreements and related agreements and who have lost their right to free coal as a result of the termination of collective agreements and agreements relating thereto. The difficult financial situation of mining companies resulted in numerous savings in mines. The management of the companies did not act well for the mine's employees, e.g. reducing mining privileges. These actions have resulted in a differentiation of mining rights for access to free coal among the group of pensioners and pensioners depending on the status of the mine they left. I must stress that we now have a situation in which pensioners who have left the mine, which has been liquidated and declared bankrupt, still have the right to free coal in the form of a monetary equivalent that ZUS pays. This is where inequality arises in accessing the right to free coal in a group of former miners. Retirists and pensioners who worked in active mines were deprived of their right to free coal as a result of the termination of collective agreements and agreements related thereto. The bill provides for e.g. payment of one-off compensation of 10 000 PLN and the possibility, after notification by the trader of the applicant at the time, to complete the missing documents within 7 days. During the meeting of the Committee on Energy and the Treasury, many proposals for amendments to the bill were submitted. I very much welcome the fact that the committee, in my opinion, adopted one of the most important proposals for change at the meeting. The amendment concerns an extension of the deadline for submitting an application from 10 days to 21 days. I personally support this amendment. The majority of those entitled to benefit are older people who often do not have access to the Internet. In addition, collecting all the necessary documents within 10 days for the elderly, would often be an insurmountable limit. The right to free coal, whether in nature or in the form of a monetary equivalent, has always been part of the remuneration of employees of mines and pensioners, is a tradition in this profession. In view of the above, it can be said that the bill meets the expectations of the mining environment. About Free and Solidarni will vote in favour of further proceding the bill.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-12-sejm-49-3.u347	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I present a report by the Extraordinary Commission on amendments to the codifications of the Government bill amending the Code of Civil Procedure Act, the Law on Sobriety Education and Against Alcoholism, and the Act on Mental Health Protection, Prints No. 1676 and 1858. I would like to remind you that the aim of this project is to introduce into the current legal system the rules on which family courts are based an order for the court curator to conduct environmental interviews in the enforcement proceedings in family and caring matters after the custody court has informed the court of an event likely to constitute a basis for the initiation of an ex officio procedure as well as at the stage of an exploratory procedure in the case of adults. The project also standardizes the conduct of an environmental interview in other specific categories for which the competent family court is the case. It is the Law of 26 October 1982 on sobriety education and combating alcoholism and the Law of 19 August 1994 on the protection of mental health, the first law in our legislation standardising matters relating to the protection of mental health. The need for these changes was created following the entry into force of the Regulation of the Minister of Justice of 25 June 2015. The Rules of Procedure of the General Courts in which there were no paragraphs concerning the conduct of environmental interviews by the curators, which was governed by the Rules of Procedure of the General Courts of 23 February 2007 previously in force. There is therefore a need to introduce legal provisions in this respect, especially since the enforcement of family and caring matters is not comprehensively governed by the provisions of the Code of Civil Procedure. Family courts are obliged to take various actions of their own motion, including prior to the formal initiation of legal proceedings. The aim is therefore to equip the court with the possibility of commissioning interviews to the court's curators after taking the information which may give rise to the initiation of an ex officio procedure. Such cases would be carried out on the register list of Nmo. The amendment also introduces environmental interviews by the probation officer in the enforcement proceedings concerning care and prosecution of adults who are wholly or partly incapacitated. These matters will be carried out on the Op register list. Two further categories of cases are cases from the Law on Sobriety Education and Against Alcoholism and cases from the Law on Mental Health, where the court's curators will also be able to conduct environmental interviews in enforcement proceedings. Those acts have not yet contained such grounds - the non-medical conditions resulting from the Article. 24 laws on sobriety education, e.g. causing a breakdown of family life, demoralizing minors, avoiding work, systematically disturbing peace or public order. An interview will therefore be necessary in order for the court to decide on the obligation to treat or change the treatment, as well as the decision to cease treatment. Under the Law on the Protection of Mental Health, this will establish indications of inability to meet life's needs on its own or to decide to send social assistance home without the consent of the person concerned. High Seym! At its meeting of 27 September this year. Two minor technical and legislative amendments have been introduced. Namely in the article. 1 in Amendment 1. to determine the situation concerning a minor has been replaced by the term "in order to gather information concerning a minor'. And in this article. 1 in Amendment 2. - in Article. 570 - the words "except for the case referred to in Article' are deleted. Article 570 (1) and without amending the rest of the substantive content. High Izbo! The Commission recommends that this proposal be adopted in its entirety. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-12-sejm-49-3.u351	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform has the honour to present its position on the report of the Extraordinary Commission on amendments to the codifications of the Government draft law amending the Code of Civil Procedure Act, the Law on Sobriety Education and Against Alcoholism and the Act on the Protection of Mental Health. The proposed law amending three legal acts introduces a solution to the Polish legal system, the purpose of which is to equip the family court with the right to instruct the court curator to conduct environmental interviews in the enforcement proceedings conducted in family and caring matters, and after the family and caring court has made information about an event which may constitute a real basis for the initiation of an ex officio procedure, as well as at the stage of an exploratory procedure in the case of adults. The interviews referred to in the proposed legal act would concern minors in the event of circumstances justifying interference in the exercise of parental authority or in the case of adults in the circumstances justifying the initiation of proceedings for the establishment of a guardian or guardian. So on the one hand, we have some actual events that justify a certain activity, and on the other hand, a man has the right to a private life, the right to respect his home mire, which are special values, subject to special protection. They are protected primarily by the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, as well as by ratified international agreements, including the Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms, signed in Rome, the International Covenant on Civil and Political Rights, opened for signature in New York, or the Convention on the Rights of the Child, adopted by the General Assembly of the United Nations. In these circumstances, there is a question of setting the right limit, a marking line separating, on the one hand, the need for interference in the interests of the natural person and, on the other hand, the need to protect those goods. It is therefore a matter of ensuring that changes to the law or to the law alone do no more harm than good. Initiating an interview by a probation officer e.g. Anonymously, it seems to raise big doubts. It is not acceptable to interfere in the sphere of family mirage when the causes of this interference could result from misunderstood human kindness. The harm caused under such circumstances may even be irreparable. The law should be a cure for problems, not poison. So here we are dealing with a two-right confrontation, two ideas, and it seems that we need to look for a golden means to solve this problem. It seems to us that the attempt to find such a solution is an amendment which is tabled by the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club to the proposed law, namely we propose to amend the wording of the article. 1 point (1) (a) b in such a way that it may be possible for a court curator to order certain interview activities when a family court takes a credible message of an event justifying the initiation of an ex officio procedure and in the course of an enforcement procedure. Here we also propose to change the literal indication of the amended provision, that is to say § 1a added to Article 570. It seems to us that the amendment we are proposing responds to, alleviates problems that could arise due to sometimes too misunderstood understanding and taking any action without a legitimate reason. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-12-sejm-49-3.u355	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club I would like to present a statement on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Code of Civil Procedure Act, the Law on education in sobriety and combating alcoholism, and the Act on the Protection of Mental Health, Prints No 1676 and 1858. The proposal concerns the introduction of provisions providing a legal basis for family courts to order the court curator to conduct environmental interviews in family and caring matters also after the judgment is delivered and after the custody court has taken information which may constitute a factual basis for the initiation of proceedings of its own motion. According to the applicants, the project standardises environmental intelligence issues in cases such as: to treat people who are addicted to alcohol, to place them in a psychiatric hospital or a social security home without consent. The Act is to enter into force 30 days after the date of the announcement. The Extraordinary Commission on Codification Changes in its work made only stylistic amendments, not substantive amendments, e.g. instead of: in order to determine the situation concerning the minor and his environment, the following shall appear: in order to gather legal information for family courts to order the court curator to conduct environmental interviews in family and caring matters also after the decision to be enforced and after the court has taken custody of the information likely to constitute... As the applicants state in the explanatory memorandum, the information collected by the curator will help the court to decide whether to rehab or stay in a psychiatric hospital, and will also help to remove the question of interpretation of environmental interviews and thus fill the existing legal gap. However, the project also has numerous disadvantages, e.g. It interferes with the sphere of human rights and freedom, which is the basis for our functioning. The project lacks the definition of the categories of cases and types of proceedings in which environmental interviews can be commissioned, and the criteria for environmental interviews regulation are unclear. In addition, a custody court in matters relating to the custody of the persons who have been deprived of their rights or are competent to conduct an environmental interview without referring the case to the judicial team. ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-12-sejm-49-3.u357	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On the basis of the proposed draft regulation, the guardians will be able to instruct the court to conduct environmental interviews in enforcement proceedings in custody cases and after the custody court has informed the court of the event which may give rise to ex officio proceedings (before its formal initiation) and at the stage of the investigation in the case of adults. After the change, the court's curators will also be able to conduct environmental interviews in cases concerning the use of compulsory treatment of alcohol addicts, as well as the placement of a person in a psychiatric hospital or admission to a social assistance home without their consent. The information collected by the court curator during the environmental interview will help the court assess whether there are non-medical reasons for deciding whether to take alcohol-dependent treatment (family life distribution, demoralizing minors, avoiding work, disturbing peace or public order) or to place a person without her consent in a psychiatric hospital (inability to exist alone) or to send a person to the home of social assistance without her consent (necessary care by others). High Izbo! Existing rules, i.e. the law of 27 July 2001 on guardians of the courts does not specify precisely the categories of cases and the type of proceedings in which the court or judge is entitled to order a professional curator to conduct an environmental interview. In view of this, the proposal in question, which sets out the rules in this regard, needs to be assessed positively. The conduct of an interview at the place of residence or residence of the person concerned is the interference of the State (a public officer, who is a court curator) in the sphere of rights and freedoms protected by both national and international law. Unclear criteria for such interference, based solely on interpretation rules and not on legislation, may raise doubts from the point of view of the standards of the rule of law. This project will clarify the criteria for a court's commissioning an environmental interview, which will undoubtedly improve the standards for the application of law in this area. The implementation of the responsibilities of care courts resulting from supervision of care requires specific tools. Without the knowledge of the living conditions of a disabled person or disabled person, including his or her living and social situation, it is not possible to verify the manner in which he or she is taken care of and the circumstances and, in the past, to determine correctly the extent of the custody of the guardian or guardian for that person. In view of the impact of the proposed regulation on the improvement of the operation of courts and curators, it should be considered beneficial for the efficiency of the supervision system in Poland.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-12-sejm-49-3.u378	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the government draft law amending the Foreigners Act and some other laws. The rules introduced are designed to help us manage migration consciously in our country and to create a better legal framework for the reception of foreigners. I believe that the introduction of facilitations in granting temporary residence and work permits and on permanent residence of workers in professions desirable on the Polish market will have a positive impact on the labour market and will reduce labour shortages in these sectors of the Polish economy. Introduction of optional limits by division, among others. to voivodships, professions and types of agreements will allow for future control of the situation and response to changing needs. Most importantly, this will prevent the emergence of negative developments in the area of the labour market. The provisions of the contested law extend this to a situation where the permanent residence permit is preceded by refugee status, subsidiary protection and humanitarian residence consent. The amendment introduced in this Article corresponds to the principle of equal treatment against the law. During the meeting of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, an extraordinary subcommittee was set up to work on a draft law on foreigners. However, it should be stressed that no substantive amendments were adopted during the subcommittee meeting. It was only during the committee's work that many amendments were tabled. ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-12-sejm-49-3.u380	Thank you very much, Marshal. Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I am very pleased to be able, on behalf of the Committee on European Union Affairs, for the fourth time as chairman of this committee, to present a report and present a draft resolution of the Sejm on the participation of the Republic of Poland in the work of the European Union between January and June 2017 chaired by Malta in the Council of the European Union. Information from the Council of Ministers on Poland's activities during the Maltese Presidency was adopted by the Committee for European Affairs on 6 July and forwarded to the High House on 7 July. The government's information, as in previous years, was prepared in cooperation with all ministries and central offices, and the Ministry of Foreign Affairs is responsible for its presentation. It is already 27 half-yearly report of the Council of Ministers for the Sejm and the Senate of the Republic of Poland from participation in the work of the European Union during subsequent periods of the presidency. The Commission for European Union Affairs considered the information provided in print 1723 at its 156th meeting on 11 October 2017. The information on behalf of the Council of Ministers was presented by Minister Konrad Szymański, State Secretary at the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. The Minister presented the progress and achievements of the Maltese Presidency through its priorities and key issues from Poland's point of view. The Maltese Presidency focused on the following issues: migration, internal market, security, social inclusion, neighbourhood policy and maritime policy. I would like to say that during the Maltese Presidency it was also the 60th anniversary of the signing of the Treaties of Rome. Personally, I am pleased to mention this anniversary about the parliamentary dimension. I had the pleasure to take part and speak on behalf of the Polish Sejm at the extraordinary summit of the Presidents of the European Union Parliaments in Rome organized on the occasion of this unique anniversary. At the government level, on the other hand, the end of the 60th anniversary of the Treaties of Rome was the signing of a declaration by the Heads of Government and States. I would like to emphasise in particular that we welcome the inclusion in the Polish declaration of the request to strengthen the role of Member States by making national parliaments more involved in decision-making by the European Union. Returning to the key points of the Presidency, the Maltese Presidency continued to seek constructive solutions on migration. The Presidency's activities focused primarily on the adoption of new ways of cooperation on the central Mediterranean route. In addition, in order to reduce illegal migratory flows to the European Union, Union action was aimed at stepping up cooperation with the countries of North Africa. The Presidency was also working on a common European asylum system to establish a mandatory and automatic allocation mechanism for applicants for international protection. Today we know that the principles of this system have been rejected because action within the European Union cannot be based on coercion and pressure on the Member States. The second priority of the Presidency, the internal market, was focused on the implementation of the Single Market Strategy and the Digital Single Market Strategy. I would like to remind you that this was a time of both positive and negative actions from the point of view of Poland. As a success, we can jointly consider the agreements that have led to the abolition of roaming charges since June 2017. On the other hand, a very important topic for Poland was published at the end of November 2016, a package of legislative proposals on electricity, i.e. the so-called. winter package. The new package of documents proposed by the European Commission raises concerns about the proposed legislation which favours gas in power market support mechanisms and eliminates coal-based technologies. In addition, direct discrimination against coal installations can be observed, there is no systematic increase in their efficiency and the possibility of using clean coal technologies. It also raises concerns about how to implement this solution without assessing the impact of its regulation. At this point, I would like to point out that, while implementing the possibilities of parliamentary diplomacy, I have written to the 16 chairmen of the committee on European national Parliaments, calling for action to adopt a reasoned opinion on the solutions contained in the draft regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council on the internal electricity market, which is so-called. the winter package. These active actions resulted in the adoption by the Sejm of a reasoned opinion on 11 May 2017, i.e. the adoption of a resolution on the recognition of the draft Regulation COM (2016) 861 as incompatible with the principle of subsidiarity. Reservations concerning the compatibility of the draft regulation with the principle of subsidiarity were notified in the form of reasoned opinions by parliamentary chambers: from Poland - Senate, from Austria - Federal Council, from the Czech Republic - Chamber of Poselska, from Germany - Bundestag and Bundesrat, from Hungary - National Assembly, from Romania - Chamber of Deputies and Senate and from Spain - General Cortesis. However, at least 19 of the 56 votes allocated to national Parliaments had to be met to initiate the procedure. In the case of draft regulation 861, the number of votes was 12, so unfortunately the required voting threshold has not been reached. However, I would like to assure you that until the documents are adopted, within the framework of the European Union's Committee on Affairs, we will continue to discuss and develop a Polish position. The projects proposed by the European Commission lead to profound changes in the shape of the energy sector. I would like to stress that the new regulations must not lead to discrimination against coal sources, which for countries such as Poland are not only the basis of energy mix, but also the foundation of energy security. We also continue to act by explaining our partners, and within V4, and in the Baltic States, our position on coal. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Another challenge faced by the Maltese Presidency was security issues. The priority was to strengthen cooperation with the European External Action Service in the implementation of the European Global Strategy, to counter terrorism and to establish an EU entry and exit system for third-country nationals. As always, the European Union's defence and security policies have been complementary to NATO activities. With regard to the Social Agenda, negotiations on the draft revision of the Posting of Workers Directive in the framework of the provision of services are still ongoing. During the presidency of Malta to the Council of the European Union, the committee held 39 meetings, examined 49 European Union documents, including 60 draft legislative acts, adopted five opinions, including four opinions on Union documents. In the case of two documents, the committee found that the draft legislative act was not compatible with the principle of subsidiarity, it was a draft regulation on the internal electricity market and a draft directive on the balance between working life and private parents and carers. The Permanent Sub-Committee on the use of funds originating in the European Union and the Emergency Sub-Committee on Energy Policy of the European Union remain active within the European Union. ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-12-sejm-49-3.u383	Thank you very much. Please take the floor of the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Mr Konrad Szymański, to present the information. Please, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-10-12-sejm-49-3.u384	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, It is extremely nice for the fourth time for me to present to the Polish Parliament information about Polish policy during the next Presidency, which ended in the middle of this year, the Maltese Presidency. A very detailed document prepared by the Ministry of Foreign Affairs has been forwarded to you by the appropriate means. I would like to focus on those issues which, in my opinion, were the most important during these six months from the point of view of Polish politics. Of course, the natural key challenge faced by Malta during its presidency was the issue of the still unconvinced migration crisis. Malta, like Poland, is a border country managing the external border of the European Union, which is also a country whose border is a very sensitive section of the external border of the European Union, where we are still dealing with a non-determinated, uncontrolled wave of migration, which, of course, has direct consequences for the countries there, while the crisis itself clearly also affects the political stability of the European Union itself, which is why it is also important from a Polish point of view. With the enormous efforts of the Maltese administration and in a spirit of good cooperation with the Member States at the very beginning of this Presidency, we have adopted the La Valletta Declaration, which sets the course, rhythm, sets out further initiatives that are being implemented within the European Union on the external dimension of migration policy. First of all, they are. These are, of course, issues relating to the direct protection of the external border, not only to the technical dimension, to the physical protection of the border, in particular in such difficult circumstances as the maritime border, but also to equipment, as far as the external border is concerned, to instruments which allow all Member States to have access to information on how migration flows are carried out, personal traffic at the external borders of the Union. This is an extremely important aspect of building not only a sense of control as regards the external border, but also a sense of credibility of the Schengen system. Without proper protection of the external border, the Schengen system will be washed up, and confidence will also be washed up in countries that are completely deep within the European Union. This declaration concerns, in particular, cooperation in the mid-Med section, which is where Malta lies. To this day, due to political difficulties in Libya, it is difficult to repeat the successful experience of the European Union-Turkey agreement, but the direction of these changes is fully supported by the Polish Government. These are really the only instruments that can bring the desired, I believe, state of full control of the external border and migration movement in the European Union. These plans, by involving, of course, relevant international institutions, in particular the United Nations High Commissioner for Refugees, including the International Organisation for Migration, assume the growing involvement of the European Union in humanitarian aid and development aid, in investment policy, in building the resilience of countries that are source countries, countries of origin for migration, as well as transit countries for migrants. All this very ambitious plan assumes that the European Union in this part of the world, where migratory tension is born, where destabilisation is born causing a million-dollar scale when it comes to leaving homes by millions of people, that the European Union will be in this region of the world, has become increasingly involved in these areas. Poland is also ready to engage, not only financially, but also personally, technically and certainly politically - this is already happening - in these activities of the European Union, which are widely supported by all Member States, because all Member States, I believe, clearly see their own interest in ensuring that the external borders of the European Union are controlled so that migration policy is controlled, predictable, that it does not raise legitimate concerns today as to whether we are able to control our own territory. This universal agreement does not exist in the European Union around other elements of the response to the migration crisis, in particular as regards the deep reform of the asylum system, the deep reform of the Dublin Regulation. Here Poland's position is equally clear. Poland will not accept that the European Union should take over the competence of Member States in border management and control of migration flows, including in the control and distribution of international care to individual groups to which this care should be taken. The Polish State is of the opinion that each Member State of the European Union should not only have responsibility, but should also have instruments to control this policy on its own, so we do not agree that even in a layer, even partially, these powers should be transferred to the level of supranational institutions. This is linked to our deep conviction that this is the responsibility of the State for security, that this is the responsibility of the State for migration, but also because such instruments, such solutions, which have recently ended pilots, do not have any expected effect. Any form of sharing responsibility for migratory pressures has not brought any significant effect to this day, has not brought relief to anyone interested, has certainly not given the impression that Europe is regaining control over its borders, over its territory. So we have at least two very serious arguments not to go on the path of failed pilotage, which was the decision to relocate 160 000. the people to whom it belongs - perhaps - international protection in the world. The second aspect, however important from a Polish point of view, is the internal market policy. Malta has made far-reaching declarations on this issue, declarations on the implementation of the internal market strategy as far as possible. The effects are quite limited. In the European Union, we see clearly that there is a great deal of tension on the basis of what the internal market should look like and what role the internal market should play. We have, so far, an unprecedented scale of protectionism in individual Member States, which is also slowly shifting to institutions which are Treaty-led to defend the integrity of the common market. Nevertheless, we appreciate the work, the start of work on a relatively modest but important legislative initiative, which is the e-card. The aim of the e-card is to significantly reduce administrative burdens, bureaucratic burdens, which are particularly important for small and medium-sized enterprises, so that they can compete more effectively in the Community market for services. We would like this initiative to be much more ambitious to be applied more widely. On the other hand, we appreciate that although this small step can be taken towards the implementation of the internal market strategy. At the same time, we had a very serious tension with an initiative that goes in a completely different direction. I am referring here to the ongoing work on the Posting of Work Directive. The lack of a proper compromise that would reconcile the integrity of the common market and equal access to the Community market for all with legitimate expectations that the work and the people doing it should be adequately protected, the absence of such a compromise, the lack of such an opportunity forced us to block work at Council level and reject the possibility of adopting a general approach on this directive, which is still the subject of not only the very intensive negotiations of the labour ministers, but also the political dialogue of the Heads of Government. On issues that are related to the Community market, although they mainly affect energy policy issues, I would include successful negotiations on the Security of Gas Supply Regulation and successful negotiations that have brought us a clear improvement in the transparency of intergovernmental agreements on energy. These are the issues that are really building the resilience of the Community market to the possible pressure of external partners, they are building our trading position, negotiating in this very sensitive area, especially for our part of Europe, the area of the energy market, but in fact they are producing an effect in the form of increasing energy security also of Poland. The third aspect of the internal market, which was important for the Maltese Presidency and is certainly crucial for Poland's policy today, is the development of the digital market. For this reason, Prime Minister Beata Szydło was one of the initiators, the main initiator of the letter of prime ministers of 17 Member States who decided to ask the European Council to speed up legislative work in all areas of the Community market, not only on issues related to the digital market, such as contract law or copyright, but also on infrastructure issues. The role of the European Union in speeding up the deployment of fibre infrastructure, the infrastructure needed to build the fifth generation network, and ultimately the management of spectrum must be urgently defined, without which the European Union will remain a fragmented market, a regulatoryly inconsistent market, a market where the rapidly growing digital market in the world will have difficulties in its full development. This is one of the invaluable and untapped potentials of the Community market, in addition to the market for services, which should be addressed in an appropriate legislative manner. Many legislative proposals have been waiting too long to be properly considered. And it will be a matter of persistent pressure from Poland to speed things up. At the same time, when we returned to energy issues, we assumed that negotiations could be made with the Russian Federation on the legal regime that should be governed by the initiative, Nord Stream investment 2. From a Polish point of view, we cannot rule out that an international agreement that would define a legal regime for this investment could be a helpful instrument. However, the objective of this agreement can only be to fully implement the EU legal order. The international agreement cannot be a gateway to the fact that, in line with the intentions of certain countries and institutions, this investment enjoys advantages on the Community market in the form of de facto exemptions from the rules to which all investors in the energy infrastructure market are subject. This case is ongoing, but it started then and I think it should be at the centre of Polish policy as long as it is agreed. In the area of social policy, I have already mentioned one extremely unsuccessful initiative - an initiative of the Posting of Labour Directive, which is actually trying to adapt the Community market to the needs of only one small group of countries, those countries which are having problems with their competitiveness in the service market, those countries which are also having internal problems with the necessary reforms. This is a very bad example of the adaptation of the Union, of the reform of the Union, which is somewhat needed, of the adaptation that is moving towards only to meet the needs of a certain group of countries, if not to say, one country in the European Union. In the case of social policy, a much broader issue was the start of work on the interinstitutional proclamation on the pillar, the European Pillar of Social Rights. This office, as you are well aware, is extremely attached to social solidarity and has proved this by many decisions, including this House, of a very measurable nature, also of a budgetary nature. This is not a distant subject. On the other hand, we do not see any grounds to activate the European Union's social policy legislation. Member States have a very defined, often inherited social policy system for decades, which corresponds to their own ideas of social solidarity, which meets their own social needs. We do not see the basis for the European Pillar of Social Rights as a substitute legal basis for the legislative activation of the European Commission, which unfortunately can be expected from the European Commission. Therefore, ongoing work on interinstitutional proclamation from a Polish point of view will focus not only on the wording of this proclamation, but also on the institutional role and legal role that this proclamation could possibly play. Another important aspect from a Polish point of view, which at that time played a role, which is gaining momentum today - perhaps it is in the course of final agreements - is of course the issue of security. Europe rightly pointed out that, as we adapt to the new challenges, we should, as the European Union and as Europe, do more for our security, because our security is today - in different parts of the Union in different ways, but unprecedentedly - at risk. Our security hasn't been as much a question mark as it is today because of what's going on in the South and what's going on in the East. So there are good reasons for the European Union to do more about security than it has done so far. A very good background, the context for such an activation of the European Union in the field of security, is the NATO agreement between the European Union and NATO at the NATO Summit, which assumes that synergies and cooperation with NATO will be the basis for building a European identity in the field of security and defence, rather than building initiatives alongside or, worse still, initiatives which would have a conflict vector in view of this fundamental framework of security of the West, of course NATO. Poland stressed this aspect in the political discussion, also at the European Council summits, but this is not the only criterion by which we look at the development of the European Union's security and defence policy. These two additional criteria are the need to maintain a geostrategic balance, as well as the availability, full and equal availability of financial resources, support for industrial policy, defence industry for all those who want to further develop the defence industry, and Poland to such countries certainly belongs. Turning to the question of geostrategic balance, I would like to stress that the very fact that we are building some policy on the basis of common resources, in this case defence policy, is not a value in itself. The value of this policy is whether it responds equally to all the risks that Member States feel in an uneven and very diverse way. The geostrategic situation of the Mediterranean countries is fundamentally different from the geostrategic situation of the countries of our part of Europe and we expect the policy that will be built on the basis of our common resources to respond equally to the threats that the European Union or Europe is experiencing today as such. The last aspect of the regular agenda of this Presidency, which I would like to draw attention to, because here too our paths with Malta are intersecting, is the issue of the neighbourhood policy. The neighbourhood policy, as it had been thought before, is experiencing a huge crisis not because of its own dysfunctions - although there are probably many things that could be done better here - but because of the very negative developments in our neighbourhood: and in the South, and in the East. For various reasons, the countries covered by the neighbourhood policy have not been able or unable to fully meet the expectations they themselves had in relation to the European Union's neighbourhood policy, nor are they able to meet the European expectations for them. Most often it is not their own merit. Most often it is a question of external interference. The most clear example of this is, of course, Ukraine, which, along with its promotion, along with its progress on the European road under the logic of the neighbourhood policy, has been the subject of increasingly aggressive behaviour of the Russian Federation, which did not hide that it was taking on the neighbourhood policy, a very soft, mild and peaceful European Union policy, as a confrontation policy against its own unjustified expectations for this part of Europe. Like Malta, we expect the European Union's neighbourhood policy to be adapted to these new conditions and to respond to the experiences we have gathered in recent years. This means that instruments need to be differentiated within the framework of the neighbourhood policy, it should be noted that the Member States covered by the neighbourhood policy are in very different positions today, and that they also have very different expectations. Countries that are willing to move forward on the European road should have such an offer. Countries which, like Ukraine and Georgia, have come a very long and painful way - a path that is difficult also for internal policy - and have come to the stage of not only the introduction of visa-free movement, but also the signing of association agreements, the signing of free trade agreements - should be able to expect more today, should meet with a proportionate response from the European Union. Their European aspirations should not be extinguished because they have already paid a huge price for it today. I would like to mention at the very end of this speech, this relationship, this information, two issues which were not directly related to the agenda of the Maltese Presidency, but were very important and took place during its duration. The first of these cases is, of course, the Brexit notification, the notification of the intention to leave the UK from the European Union. This happened exactly halfway through this Presidency. This notification resulted in a very rapid response from the European Council, which prepared a recommendation, and a very rapid response from the European Commission, which prepared an appropriate mandate, in line with the recommendations, which fulfils all Polish expectations of this process. With these expectations, of course, the rights of our citizens today and tomorrow, as soon as the United Kingdom leaves the European Union, play a key role. Their protection must be detailed, must have a very strong legal basis, international law and must have institutional guarantees of compliance with this agreement in the future, even in the distant future. This is the subject of advanced discussions today, in which we can talk about very far-reaching progress. It's about the union-British talks. The second important, no less important, is, of course, the financial clearance. This settlement does not apply to any future scenarios, nor does it apply to any UK punishment for a sovereign decision in the end. On the other hand, it concerns all commitments that have grown in the course of the long-term long-term membership of the United Kingdom in the European Union. But this is not the end of the list of Polish expectations when it comes to Brexit. Poland is equally determined and equally interested in ensuring that the UK's future relations with the European Union are as close as possible. This lies in our best interest in Poland and, I think, in Europe, that Brexit is not a negative scenario, it is not a scenario of pushing Britain away from all those things that we can do together, apart from membership. Most of our international trade, security as external as well as internal security challenges remain with us regardless of whether the United Kingdom is a member of the European Union or whether it ceases to be a member of the European Union. This is the reason why Poland retains its offensive position not only in terms of what is a matter of the past, which is a matter of UK membership, which is a question of the necessary settlement of this departure of roads, but also about the future, as far as possible ambitious, as close cooperation in all possible areas that will bring mutual benefits to the parties. The second point I wanted to draw attention to, of course, is the 60th anniversary of the Treaties of Rome. This was a very good opportunity for the Heads of Government to draw attention to what has so far happened in the field of European integration. This story was not entirely the history of Poland, but it is our heritage. In this case the Polish voice sounded very strongly. We have stressed in this past how the internal market, its integrity and the market, which is supposed to be the most sustainable, sustainable achievement of the integration process, play a crucial role. We have drawn attention to the need, in particular in view of the migration crisis, the preservation of Schengen integrity and heritage, the functionality of the Schengen area, which today does not work to the same extent, as well as efficiently on each section. Here Poland is an example of a country where there is no disturbance in this respect, but not far from here, in our part of Europe, the Schengen system has encountered significant dysfunctions. And the third point that we have highlighted so far is the obvious success of enlargement. The enlargement, and in particular the largest enlargements, which moved the European Union's external border far east from the point of view of Lisbon, Paris or even Berlin, was a huge success of integration. The future can be built on this success. Of course, the work on the 60-year declaration of the Treaties of Rome was not limited to a summary of the past. It was and remains a very hot time, so they were largely turned to the future. What we wanted to achieve, what we wanted to draw attention to on this occasion, is not only building on what is an obvious success of integration, enlargement, the common market or Schengen, but also wanting to point out that none of the European problems will be overcome, will not be resolved by any division, that the institutional division of the European Union, although possible and perhaps for some is even a comfortable perspective, will not solve any of our common problems. On the contrary, it will bring new problems. Europe will simply be weaker by division, so it will be less, less effectively defending its security interests, its market interests, its commercial interests, economic, political interests, whatever. There is no case in history that a smaller institution is more influential. And this is the thing that, I think, most strongly sounded at that time and remains valid because the success of adopting a common - I think it is satisfying for everyone to the same extent - a declaration related to the 60-year Treaty of Rome unfortunately does not close the issue of uncertainty about the future of the European Union. But this matter is ahead of us and will probably come back on the occasion of further government information on the next European Union Presidency. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
